### Q39. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The tangent to the curve <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></math>, parallel to the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></math>, also passes through the point :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of line parallel to given line can be taken as<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi></math>.</p><p>Now, this line touches the curve <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></math> hence, roots of the equation</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> must be equal.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>D</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#183;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>29</mn></math></p><p>Hence, equation of tangent is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>29</mn></math>.</p><p>Clearly, it passes through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>.</p>

</details>

---

### Q40. JEE Main 2019 (10 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The tangent to the curve, <math><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msup></math> passing through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> also passes through the point:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>2</mn><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>3</mn><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>2</mn><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>6</mn><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>2</mn><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given <math><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> at <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>e</mi></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> Equation of tangent is <math><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>e</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>e</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>, which passes through <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>.

</details>

---

### Q41. JEE Main 2019 (10 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A helicopter is flying along the curve given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math> A soldier positioned at the point <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>, who wants to shoot down the helicopter when it is nearest to him. Then this nearest distance is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mi> </mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>We have</p><p> <math><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msqrt><mi>x</mi></msqrt></math>

Let the point on curve be <math><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>7</mn><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mfenced></math> and given point be <math><mi>A</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>.

For nearest point normal at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> passes through <math><mi>A</mi></math>.

So, the slope of line <math><mi>A</mi><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo></math> Slope of normal at <math><mi>P</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msubsup><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msubsup><mrow><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msub><msub><mfenced close="|" open="" separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></msub><mi>&#160;</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>3</mn><msqrt><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>3</mn><msubsup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>3</mn><msubsup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>, is not possible as <math><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

Hence, point <math><mi>P</mi></math> is <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mn>7</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>.

So, <math><mi>A</mi><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>36</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>27</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math> units.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q42. JEE Main 2019 (10 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The shortest distance between the point <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and the curve <math><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi>x</mi></msqrt><mo>,</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> , is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let <math><mi>P</mi></math> be the nearest to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math> then normal at <math><mi>P</mi></math> will pass through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Any point on the parabola <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></math> can be taken as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Let, co-ordinates of <math><mi>P</mi></math> on the curve <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi>x</mi></msqrt><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi></math> can be taken as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/dzbwlsxafjixssnhafbrvaijgrorbfglbptkeyrlkxyinkcznk.png" />
The equation of the normal to the parabola <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></math> at a point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>t</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>a</mi><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Hence, the equation of normal to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi>x</mi></msqrt></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> is <math><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>t</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

This line passes through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math> hence 
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>t</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>t</mi><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math> but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math> is rejected as we have to take point in the first quadrant.</p><p>Thus, the points are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and the distances of these points from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> are respectively</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> units.

Hence, nearest point is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and the minimum distance is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> units.</p>

</details>

---

### Q43. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If <math><mi>&#952;</mi></math> denotes the acute angle between the curves, <math><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> and <math><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> at a point of their intersection, then <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfenced></math> is equal to:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>17</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>17</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>15</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>15</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Intersection points of given curves are  <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>±</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math>  for  <math><msup><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msup></math>  curve  <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi></math> 

         <math><mo>∴</mo><msub><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>  or  <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> 

        For  <math><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></msup></math>  curve,  <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi></math> 

         <math><mo>∴</mo><msub><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></math>  or  <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> 

         <math><mo>∴</mo></math>   <math><mo>|</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi>θ</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>  or  <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> 

         <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>15</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>  (In both cases)

</details>

### Q44. JEE Main 2018 (08 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the curves <math><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>9</mn><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn></math> intersect each other at right angles, then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>b</mi></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>6</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>4</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given, curves <math><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>x</mi></math> and <math><mn>9</mn><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn></math>
Let the point of intersection of curves be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math> So,
<math><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math>
<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mn>18</mn><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi><msub><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>b</mi><msub><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>

Since both the curve intersect at right angle.

<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>b</mi><msub><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>27</mn><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>b</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></math>   (Since <math><msubsup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math>)

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>27</mn><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>b</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>6</mn><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>27</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q45. JEE Main 2017 (08 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The tangent at the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math> to the curve, <math><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> does not pass through the point:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>8</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given curve <math><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>y</mi></math></p><p>Differentiate both sides w.r.t  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>,

<math> <mo>&#8658;</mo> </math> <math><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>.</mo><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math> <mo>&#8658;</mo> </math> <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>.</mo><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>.</mo><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math> <mo>&#8658;</mo> </math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced close="|" open="" separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>14</mn><mn>12</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>

Equation of tangent, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>26</mn></math>, which doesn&#39;t pass through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>.</p>

</details>

---

### Q46. JEE Main 2017 (02 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The normal to the curve  <math><mi>y</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn></math> at the point where the curve intersects the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math>-axis passes through the point:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>To find the point where curve intersects the y-axis 

Put, <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, we get</p><p> <math><mn>6</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

Hence, the point is (0, 1).

On differentiating

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

Put, <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>, we get

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> Slope of normal <math><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

Equation of normal at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

Point <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> satisfy the normal.</p>

</details>

### Q47. JEE Main 2016 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let C be a curve given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>x</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><msqrt><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> is a point on C, such that the tangent at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> has slope <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>, then a point through which the normal at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> passes, is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>x</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><msqrt><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math></p><p>So, <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> <math><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math>

So, <math><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>

Equation of normal at  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

i.e., <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> <math><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>17</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Hence only option <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is satisfying the equation.</p>

</details>

---

### Q48. JEE Main 2016 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the tangent at a point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>, with parameter <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi></math>, on the curve <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>,</mo></math> meets the curve again at a point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></math>, then the coordinates of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></math> are :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>16</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><mn>64</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given <math><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>8</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>t</mi></math> (slope of tangent at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>)

Let <math><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>8</mn><msup><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

Slope of <math><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>t</mi></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><msup><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>t</mi></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> <math><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>t</mi><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></math>  or  <math><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> <math><mi>Q</mi><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

</details>

### Q49. JEE Main 2016 (03 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Consider <math><mi>f</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>x</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math> A normal to <math><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>f</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> at <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> also passes through the point

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>f</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>x</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><msup><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#8242;</mi></mrow></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>.</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

at <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><msup><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#8242;</mi></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>.</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

Slope of tangent <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

So slope of normal <math><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

Also at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8289;</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math> .

So equation of the Normal would be <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

It passes through <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

</details>

### Q50. JEE Main 2015 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of a normal to the curve, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>sin</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mi>sin</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mtext>at</mtext><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi> </mi><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi> </mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi> </mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi> </mi><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi> </mi><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi></mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></math>

If <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>0</mi><mo>,&#160;</mo></math>then <math><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>0</mi></math>

So point is <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

Slope of tangent <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

at <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>  <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

Slope of tangent <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

Slope of Normal <math><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>&#8756;&#160;</mo></math> equation of normal <math><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

</details>

---

### Q51. JEE Main 2015 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The distance from the origin, of the normal to the curve, <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>t</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>t</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>4</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>2</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mn>2</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>At <math> <mrow> <mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mi>&#960;</mi> <mn>4</mn> </mfrac></mrow></math>,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>.</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>

<math><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#8901;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>-</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>t</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>t</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>&#8901;</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>t</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></mrow></math>  at <math><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan&#160;</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> .</p><p>Slope of tangent is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> &amp; therefore slope of normal would be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>.  

Equation of normal  <math><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi></math></p><p>Distance from origin <math><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q52. JEE Main 2015 (04 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The normal to the curve <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Meets the curve again in the fourth quadrant
- **(B)** Does not meet the curve again
- **(C)** Meets the curve again in the second quadrant
- **(D)** Meets the curve again in the third quadrant

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Meets the curve again in the fourth quadrant**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

Pair of straight lines passing through the origin.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>  or  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

Normal exists at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> which is on <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;</mo> </mrow> </math>  The slope of normal at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math> 

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> </mrow> </math>  Equation of normal will be

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>    <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

Now, find the point of intersection with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>. 

   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>
  ----------------
   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=-1,&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=3</mo></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> lies in the fourth quadrant.

</details>

### Q53. JEE Main 2014 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

For the curve $\mathrm{y}=3 \sin \theta \cos \theta, \mathrm{x}=\mathrm{e}^\theta \sin \theta$, $0 \leq \theta \leq \pi$, the tangent is parallel to $\mathrm{x}$-axis when $\theta$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{3 \pi}{4}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\pi}{2}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\pi}{4}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\pi}{6}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{\pi}{4}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given, $y=3 \sin \theta \cdot \cos \theta$
$$
\begin{aligned}
&\frac{d y}{d \theta}=3[\sin \theta(-\sin \theta)+\cos \theta(\cos \theta)] \\
&\frac{d y}{d \theta}=3\left[\cos ^2 \theta-\sin ^2 \theta\right]=3 \cos 2 \theta . \\
&\text { and } x=\mathrm{e}^\theta \sin \theta \\
&\frac{d x}{d \theta}=e^\theta \cos \theta+\sin \theta e^\theta \\
&\frac{d x}{d \theta}=e^\theta(\sin \theta+\cos \theta)
\end{aligned}
$$
Dividing (1) by (2)
$$
\begin{gathered}
\frac{d y}{d x}=\frac{3 \cos 2 \theta}{e^\theta(\sin \theta+\cos \theta)}=\frac{3\left(\cos ^2 \theta-\sin ^2 \theta\right)}{e^\theta(\sin \theta+\cos \theta)} \\
\frac{d y}{d x}=\frac{3(\cos \theta+\sin \theta)(\cos \theta-\sin \theta)}{e^\theta(\sin \theta+\cos \theta)} \\
\frac{d y}{d x}=\frac{3(\cos \theta-\sin \theta)}{e^\theta}
\end{gathered}
$$
Given tangent is parallel to $x$-axis then
$\frac{d y}{d x}=0$
$0=\frac{3(\cos \theta-\sin \theta)}{e^\theta}$
or $\cos \theta-\sin \theta=0 \Rightarrow \cos \theta=\sin \theta$
$\Rightarrow \tan \theta=1 \Rightarrow \tan \theta=\frac{\tan \pi}{4} \Rightarrow \theta=\frac{\pi}{4}$

</details>

### Q54. JEE Main 2014 (06 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The slope of the line touching both the parabolas <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>32</mn><mi>y</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>8</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced><mi>x</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>y</mi></math>

The equation of the tangent to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mi>m</mi></mfrac></math>
The equation of the tangent to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>b</mi><mi>y</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>b</mi><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

Since, they have a common tangent,

      <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>b</mi><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> </mrow> </math> The slope of the common tangent is <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>.

</details>

### Q55. JEE Main 2013 (25 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If an equation of a tangent to the curve, $y-\cos (x+f),-1-1 \leq x \leq 1+\pi$, is $x+2 y=k$ then $k$ is equal to :

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** 2
- **(C)** $\frac{\pi}{4}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\pi}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{\pi}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $y=\cos (x+y)$
$$
\Rightarrow \frac{d y}{d x}=-\sin (x+y)\left(1+\frac{d y}{d x}\right)
$$
Now, given equation of tangent is
$$
\begin{aligned}
&x+2 y=k \\
&\Rightarrow \text { Slope }=\frac{-1}{2}
\end{aligned}
$$
So, $\frac{d y}{d x}=\frac{-1}{2}$ put this value in (1), we get
$$
\begin{aligned}
&\frac{-1}{2}=-\sin (x+y)\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\right) \\
&\Rightarrow \sin (x+y)=1 \\
&\Rightarrow x+y=\frac{\pi}{2} \Rightarrow y=\frac{\pi}{2}-x
\end{aligned}
$$
Now $\frac{\pi}{2}-x=\cos (x+y)$
$$
\Rightarrow x=\frac{\pi}{2} \text { and } y=0
$$
Thus $x+2 y=k \quad \Rightarrow \frac{\pi}{2}=k$

</details>

### Q56. JEE Main 2013 (23 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If a curve passes through the point $\left(2, \frac{7}{2}\right)$ and has slope $\left(1-\frac{1}{x^2}\right)$ at any point $(x, y)$ on it, then the ordinate of the point on the curve whose abscissa is $-2$ is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-\frac{3}{2}$
- **(B)** $\frac{3}{2}$
- **(C)** $\frac{5}{2}$
- **(D)** $-\frac{5}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $-\frac{3}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \text { Slope }=\frac{d y}{d x}=1-\frac{1}{x^2} \\
& \Rightarrow \int d y=\int\left(1-\frac{1}{x^2}\right) d x \\
& \Rightarrow y=x+\frac{1}{x}+\mathrm{C}, \text { which is the equation of }
\end{aligned}
$$
the curve
since curve passes through the point
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \left(2, \frac{7}{2}\right) \\
\therefore \quad & \frac{7}{2}=2+\frac{1}{2}+C \Rightarrow C=1 \\
\therefore \quad & y=x+\frac{1}{x}+1
\end{aligned}
$$
when $x=-2$, then $y=-2+\frac{1}{-2}+1=\frac{-3}{2}$

</details>

### Q57. JEE Main 2013 (23 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the curves $\frac{x^2}{\alpha}+\frac{y^2}{4}=1$ and $y^3=16 x$ intersect at right angles, then a value of $\alpha$ is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** $\frac{4}{3}$
- **(C)** $\frac{1}{2}$
- **(D)** $\frac{3}{4}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{4}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \text { } \frac{x^2}{\alpha}+\frac{y^2}{4}=1 \Rightarrow \frac{2 x}{\alpha}+\frac{2 y}{4} \cdot \frac{d y}{d x}=0 \\
& \Rightarrow \frac{d y}{d x}=\frac{-4 x}{\alpha y} \\
& y^3=16 x \Rightarrow 3 y^2 \cdot \frac{d y}{d x}=16 \Rightarrow \frac{d y}{d x}=\frac{16}{3 y^2} \ldots
\end{aligned}
$$
Since curves intersects at right angles
$$
\therefore \frac{-4 x}{\alpha y} \times \frac{16}{3 y^2}=-1 \Rightarrow 3 \alpha y^3=64 x
$$

</details>

### Q58. JEE Main 2013 (07 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The intercepts on the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>-axis made by tangents to the curve, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><munderover><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><mi>x</mi></munderover><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>t</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>,</mo></math> which are parallel to the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi></math>, are equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mo>&#177;</mo> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mo>&plusmn;</mo> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mo>±</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mo>&plusmn;</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math> <mrow> <mo>±</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><munderover><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><mi>x</mi></munderover><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mi>t</mi></mfenced><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></math>

If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, then

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><munderover><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><mi>x</mi></munderover><mi>t</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></math><br .="" br="" /><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

Ff <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, then <math> <mrow> <mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>={</mo><mtable><mtr><mtd><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>,</mi></mtd><mtd><mi>x</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>,</mi></mtd><mtd><mi>x</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>

Now, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>{</mo><mtable><mtr><mtd><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo></mtd><mtd><mi>x</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mi>,</mi></mtd><mtd><mi>x</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>

Slope of tangent <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

Hence <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

Eq. of tangent <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo></math>intercept <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>.

</details>

---

### Q59. JEE Main 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the tangent to the curve $y=x+\frac{4}{x^2}$, that is parallel to the $x$-axis, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $y=1$
- **(B)** $y=2$
- **(C)** $y=3$
- **(D)** $y=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $y=3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Parallel to $x$-axis $\quad \Rightarrow \frac{d y}{d x}=0 \quad \Rightarrow 1-\frac{8}{x^3}=0$ $\Rightarrow x=2 \quad \Rightarrow y=3$
Equation of tangent is $y-3=0(x-2) \quad \Rightarrow y-3=0$

</details>

---

### Q60. JEE Main 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The normal to a curve at $P(x, y)$ meets the $x$-axis at $G$. If the distance of $G$ from the origin is twice the abscissa of $P$, then the curve is a

**Options:**

- **(A)** ellipse
- **(B)** parabola
- **(C)** circle
- **(D)** pair of straight lines

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) ellipse**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of normal is $Y-y=-\frac{d x}{d y}(X-x)$
$\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow G \equiv\left(x+y \frac{d y}{d x}, 0\right) \\
& \left|x+y \frac{d y}{d x}\right|=|2 x| \\
& \Rightarrow y \frac{d y}{d x}=x \text { or } y \frac{d y}{d x}=-3 x \\
& y d y=x d x \text { or } y d y=-3 x d x \\
& \frac{y^2}{2}=\frac{x^2}{2}+c \text { or } \frac{y^2}{2}=-\frac{3 x^2}{2}+c \\
& x^2-y^2=-2 c \text { or } 3 x^2+y^2=2 c.
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q61. JEE Main 2005

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The normal to the curve $x=a(\cos \theta+\theta \sin \theta), y=a(\sin \theta-\theta \cos \theta)$ at any point ' $\theta$ ' is such that

**Options:**

- **(A)** it passes through the origin
- **(B)** it makes angle $\frac{\pi}{2}+\theta$ with the $x$-axis
- **(C)** it passes through $\left(\mathrm{a} \frac{\pi}{2},-\mathrm{a}\right)$
- **(D)** it is at a constant distance from the origin

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) it is at a constant distance from the origin**

**Detailed Solution:**

Clearly $\frac{\mathrm{dy}}{\mathrm{dx}}=\tan \theta \Rightarrow$ slope of normal $=-\cot \theta$
Equation of normal at ' $\theta$ ' is
$y-a(\sin \theta-\theta \cos \theta)=-\cot \theta(x-a(\cos \theta+\theta \sin \theta)$
$\Rightarrow y \sin \theta-a \sin ^2 \theta+a \theta \cos \theta \sin \theta=-x \cos \theta+a \cos ^2 \theta+a \theta \sin \theta \cos \theta$
$\Rightarrow x \cos \theta+y \sin \theta=a$
Clearly this is an equation of straight line which is at a constant distance 'a' from origin.

</details>

### Q62. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the sum of the slopes of the lines given by $x^2-2 c x y-7 y^2=0$ is four times their product, then $c$ has the value

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** $-1$
- **(C)** 2
- **(D)** $-2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 2**

**Detailed Solution:**

$m_1+m_2=-\frac{2 c}{7}$ and $m_1 m_2=-\frac{1}{7}$
$m_1+m_2=4 m_1 m_2 \quad$ (given)
$\Rightarrow c=2$

</details>

### Q63. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The normal to the curve $x=a(1+\cos \theta), y=a \sin \theta$ at ' $\theta$ ' always passes through the fixed point

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(a, 0)$
- **(B)** $(0, a)$
- **(C)** $(0,0)$
- **(D)** $(a, a)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $(a, 0)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Eliminating $\theta$, we get $(x-a)^2+y^2=a^2$.
Hence normal always pass through $(a, 0)$

</details>
